contrast between first, later, and terminal positions.","key":"A","name":"A short reprieve before an appointed time","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"A period of calm delay is granted, but it remains bounded by a specified appointment.","active_motifs":"deliberate delay and absence of haste (`quranic:root_001452:B001/m01`); a small interval (`quranic:root_001251:B001/m01`); postponement to a later time (`quranic:root_000019:B002/m01`); an appointment fixed by time or place (`quranic:root_001662:B003/m01`); a duration of time (`quranic:root_001700:B002/m01`); an event present in time (`quranic:root_001332:B001/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:11 `مَهِّلْهُمْ/قَلِيلًا`; 73:14,17 `يَوْمَ/يَوْمًا`; 73:18 `كَانَ/وَعْدُهُ`; 73:20 `ءَاخَرُونَ/يَكُونُ`","synthesis":"Delay is not cancellation but a measured opening before an appointment. “Later” and temporal occurrence keep the reprieve inside a schedule whose endpoint remains intact.","ayah_refs":["73:11","73:14","73:18","73:20"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Deferred Appointment and Irreversible Arrival","parent_semantic_invariant":"Delay belongs to a fixed schedule whose promised event ultimately arrives, occurs, and acquires the force of an accomplished act.","parent_surprising_reach":"Temporal reckoning extends into moon stations, lunar halos, star markers, weekday naming, and the contrast between first, later, and terminal positions.","key":"B","name":"Promise becoming an accomplished event","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"A verbal commitment advances from expectation through arrival to enacted fact.","active_motifs":"a promise opening expectation (`quranic:root_001662:B001/m01`); threatening promise (`quranic:root_001662:B002/m01`); coming and arrival (`quranic:root_000009:B001/m01`); calamity arriving upon a person (`quranic:root_000009:B011/m01`); return to an outcome or final meaning (`quranic:root_000067:B002/m01`); occurrence in time (`quranic:root_001332:B001/m01`); an enacted deed (`quranic:root_001167:B001/m01`); a calamitous day or event (`quranic:root_001700:B003/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:11 `أُو۟لِى`; 73:14,17 `يَوْمَ/يَوْمًا`; 73:18 `كَانَ/وَعْدُهُ/مَفْعُولًا`; 73:20 `ءَاتُ/يَكُونُ`","synthesis":"The sequence closes the distance between saying and reality. Arrival supplies the transition, occurrence supplies presence, action realizes it, and outcome names what the event finally becomes or means.","ayah_refs":["73:11","73:14","73:18","73:20"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Deferred Appointment and Irreversible Arrival","parent_semantic_invariant":"Delay belongs to a fixed schedule whose promised event ultimately arrives, occurs, and acquires the force of an accomplished act.","parent_surprising_reach":"Temporal reckoning extends into moon stations, lunar halos, star markers, weekday naming, and the contrast between first, later, and terminal positions.","key":"C","name":"Reading the celestial calendar","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"Moon position, halo, stellar mansion, and named days provide observable markers for scheduled time.","active_motifs":"the moon taking successive stations (`quranic:root_000018:B008/m01`); a lunar halo (`quranic:root_001613:B003/m01`); a lunar mansion of three stars (`quranic:root_001096:B006/m01`); a guiding mark or landmark (`quranic:root_001040:B002/m01`); the named day Tuesday (`quranic:root_000203:B006/m01`); daylight as a bounded unit (`quranic:root_001700:B001/m01`); an observable sign promising a future condition (`quranic:root_001662:B006/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:14,17 `يَوْمَ/يَوْمًا`; 73:16,19 `أَخَذْ/ٱتَّخَذَ`; 73:18 `وَعْدُهُ`; 73:20 `ثُلُثَىِ/عَلِمَ/غَفُورٌ`","synthesis":"These motifs form a practical timekeeping scene: celestial position is observed, marked, grouped, and translated into named units. The promised day is thus pressured by a latent calendar whose signs make an appointment legible.","ayah_refs":["73:14","73:16","73:18","73:20"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Failure of Earthly, Celestial, and Hydraulic Containment","parent_semantic_invariant":"Structures that normally support, cover, or channel material lose integrity and release what they hold.","parent_surprising_reach":"Mountain collapse, sky rupture, flood, runoff, and hidden erosion are variants of one containment failure across solid, aerial, and liquid media.","key":"A","name":"Mountain mass becoming poured sand","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"A hard elevated mass shakes, loses resistance, granulates, and pours away.","active_motifs":"violent shaking (`quranic:root_000545:B001/m01`); earth as the lower support (`quranic:root_000025:B001/m01`); a raised solid mountain mass (`quranic:root_000217:B001/m01`); hardness that stops excavation (`quranic:root_000217:B005/m01`); encounter with broad sand (`quranic:root_000217:B011/m01`); gathered dune material (`quranic:root_001285:B001/m01`); loose measure pouring without stability (`quranic:root_001613:B001/m01`); occurrence and transformation in time (`quranic:root_001332:B001/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:14 `تَرْجُفُ/ٱلْأَرْضُ/ٱلْجِبَالُ/كَانَتِ/كَثِيبًا/مَّهِيلًا`","synthesis":"The process is a phase change in structural behavior: rigid mass begins to oscillate, breaks into aggregate, and flows like unmeasured grain. The branch sequence gives precise mechanics to the surface simile.","ayah_refs":["73:14"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Failure of Earthly, Celestial, and Hydraulic Containment","parent_semantic_invariant":"Structures that normally support, cover, or channel material lose integrity and release what they hold.","parent_surprising_reach":"Mountain collapse, sky rupture, flood, runoff, and hidden erosion are variants of one containment failure across solid, aerial, and liquid media.","key":"B","name":"The overhead cover splitting open","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"What normally rises above and covers the world tears and exposes the space beneath.","active_motifs":"sky or overhead canopy (`quranic:root_000745:B004/m01`); opening and splitting (`quranic:root_001165:B001/m01`); the lower realm opposite the sky (`quranic:root_000025:B001/m01`); a covering darkness or sea (`quranic:root_001307:B002/m01`); basic covering and concealment (`quranic:root_001307:B001/m01`); an exposed body with no cover from the sky (`quranic:root_000994:B004/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:13 `عَذَابًا`; 73:14 `ٱلْأَرْضُ`; 73:17 `كَفَرْتُمْ`; 73:18 `ٱلسَّمَآءُ/مُنفَطِرٌ`","synthesis":"Sky and earth establish the vertical enclosure; splitting removes its upper boundary. The covering senses of `ك ف ر` sharpen the reversal: the same lexical field that hides now supplies the cover that can no longer remain closed.","ayah_refs":["73:13","73:14","73:17","73:18"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Failure of Earthly, Celestial, and Hydraulic Containment","parent_semantic_invariant":"Structures that normally support, cover, or channel material lose integrity and release what they hold.","parent_surprising_reach":"Mountain collapse, sky rupture, flood, runoff, and hidden erosion are variants of one containment failure across solid, aerial, and liquid media.","key":"C","name":"Water exceeding channels and boundaries","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"Heavy rain and imported runoff overwhelm the distinction between channel, land, and enclosing wall.","active_motifs":"an encircling flood (`quranic:root_000957:B002/m01`); heavy rain with large drops (`quranic:root_001619:B001/m01`); rain exceeding ordinary need (`quranic:root_000138:B006/m01`); runoff arriving from another land (`quranic:root_000009:B005/m01`); rain hanging between cloud and earth (`quranic:root_000672:B005/m01`); weather striking earth and vegetation (`quranic:root_000906:B013/m01`); a fortified enclosing wall (`quranic:root_000957:B010/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:14,20 `ٱلْأَرْضُ/أَرْضِ`; 73:16 `وَبِيلًا`; 73:19-20 `سَبِيلًا/سَبِيلِ`; 73:20 `ءَاتُ/يَبْتَغُونَ/يَضْرِبُونَ/طَآئِفَةٌ`","synthesis":"Rain descends, runoff arrives laterally, and flood circles the site until ordinary boundaries fail. The enclosing wall supplies the expected defense whose insufficiency makes the inundation a true collapse scene.","ayah_refs":["73:14","73:16","73:19","73:20"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Message Tested by Testimony and Enactment","parent_semantic_invariant":"A communicated claim is tested by witness, enacted fact, and the quality of its recipients’ response.","parent_surprising_reach":"Falsehood appears not only as lying but as a failed charge, deceptive cloth, rumor-induced panic, and fabricated composition.","key":"B","name":"Denial, rumor, and deceptive representation","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"Recipients reject the message while false representations circulate and generate disturbance.","active_motifs":"falsehood opposed to truth (`quranic:root_001290:B001/m01`); attributing falsehood to a speaker (`quranic:root_001290:B002/m01`); a deceptive dyed or patterned cloth (`quranic:root_001290:B009/m01`); denial and concealment of truth (`quranic:root_001307:B003/m01`); alarming or false rumor (`quranic:root_000545:B003/m01`); fabricated discourse made without a prior model (`quranic:root_001167:B004/m01`); disobedience to authority (`quranic:root_001022:B001/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:11 `ٱلْمُكَذِّبِينَ`; 73:14 `تَرْجُفُ`; 73:16 `عَصَىٰ`; 73:17 `كَفَرْتُمْ`; 73:18 `مَفْعُولًا`","synthesis":"The scene distinguishes several failures of representation: direct lying, accusing another of lying, visual deception, fabricated composition, and rumor. Disobedience is the behavioral outcome when that representational refusal is directed at an envoy.","ayah_refs":["73:11","73:14","73:16","73:17","73:18"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Message Tested by Testimony and Enactment","parent_semantic_invariant":"A communicated claim is tested by witness, enacted fact, and the quality of its recipients’ response.","parent_surprising_reach":"Falsehood appears not only as lying but as a failed charge, deceptive cloth, rumor-induced panic, and fabricated composition.","key":"C","name":"Commitment verified by action","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"A promise is judged true when a corresponding act occurs and becomes observable fact.","active_motifs":"verbal commitment (`quranic:root_001662:B001/m01`); mutual commitment (`quranic:root_001662:B004/m01`); an enacted deed (`quranic:root_001167:B001/m01`); occurrence in time (`quranic:root_001332:B001/m01`); knowledgeable disclosure (`quranic:root_001040:B001/m01`); finding an object as present (`quranic:root_001626:B001/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:14,18,20 `كَانَتِ/كَانَ/يَكُونُ`; 73:18 `وَعْدُهُ/مَفْعُولًا`; 73:20 `يَعْلَمُ/عَلِمَ/تَجِدُوهُ`","synthesis":"Promise and action are not merged: promise establishes the claim, occurrence gives it temporal presence, knowledge recognizes it, and finding supplies experiential verification. The “fulfilled promise” is therefore a completed evidentiary chain.","ayah_refs":["73:14","73:18","73:20"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Message Tested by Testimony and Enactment","parent_semantic_invariant":"A communicated claim is tested by witness, enacted fact, and the quality of its recipients’ response.","parent_surprising_reach":"Falsehood appears not only as lying but as a failed charge, deceptive cloth, rumor-induced panic, and fabricated composition.","key":"D","name":"Answer, assent, and unstable alignment","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"A recipient answers affirmatively, then either embodies submission or merely aligns with whichever side prevails.","active_motifs":"saying yes in confirmation or promise (`quranic:root_001525:B004/m01`); answering a call (`quranic:root_001573:B003/m01`); bodily gesture of submission (`quranic:root_001307:B014/m01`); submissive yielding (`quranic:root_001332:B004/m01`); siding with the victor without a settled position (`quranic:root_001434:B005/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:11 `ٱلنَّعْمَةِ`; 73:14,18,20 `كَانَتِ/كَانَ/يَكُونُ`; 73:17 `كَفَرْتُمْ`; surface anchor unavailable for `quranic:root_001573:B003` because `ه ا ء` is missing from root coverage, and for `quranic:root_001434:B005` because `م ع ع` is missing from root coverage","synthesis":"Verbal yes, responsive answer, bodily deference, and inward yielding can align, but the opportunistic partisan exposes their possible separation. Recipient response is therefore tested not by assent alone but by whether word, posture, and durable position correspond.","ayah_refs":["73:11","73:14","73:17"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Compressed Life Cycle","parent_semantic_invariant":"Birth, growth, maturation, and aging can be accelerated until distant life stages collapse into one event.","parent_surprising_reach":"The child-to-grey transformation is supported by a detailed reproductive ledger of womb, afterbirth, childbirth blood, near delivery, tooth eruption, and derived new life.","key":"B","name":"Childhood turning immediately to old age","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"A young dependent is transformed into a grey-haired figure without traversing ordinary duration.","active_motifs":"offspring and child (`quranic:root_001683:B001/m01`); a recently born child (`quranic:root_001683:B004/m01`); white hair and aging (`quranic:root_000833:B001/m01`); an old man defined by looking back on youth (`quranic:root_001332:B005/m01`); a calamitous day (`quranic:root_001700:B003/m01`); transforming a person into a new state (`quranic:root_000248:B002/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:14,18,20 `كَانَتِ/كَانَ/يَكُونُ`; 73:17 `يَوْمًا/يَجْعَلُ/ٱلْوِلْدَٰنَ/شِيبًا`","synthesis":"The process is temporal compression expressed as transformation. Newborn status and retrospective old age are forced into direct adjacency by the calamitous day.","ayah_refs":["73:14","73:17"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Volition, Compulsion, and Viable Capacity","parent_semantic_invariant":"Action can arise from chosen direction, external coercion, or a capacity calibrated to what is actually possible.","parent_surprising_reach":"Choice is pressured by lexical scenes of forcing, deterrent restraint, suitability, preparation, illness, and eased passage.","key":"B","name":"Being carried, forced, or deterred","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"An external agent moves another toward an act or blocks movement through compulsion and exemplary restraint.","active_motifs":"carrying or compelling a person toward an affair (`quranic:root_000831:B003/m01`); forcing someone into an act (`quranic:root_000217:B010/m01`); coercing an obedient person into disobedience (`quranic:root_001307:B007/m01`); transforming someone’s state (`quranic:root_000248:B002/m01`); a shackle that prevents movement (`quranic:root_001554:B001/m01`); punishment that deters observers (`quranic:root_001554:B003/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:12 `أَنكَالًا`; 73:14 `ٱلْجِبَالُ`; 73:17 `كَفَرْتُمْ/يَجْعَلُ`; 73:19 `شَآءَ`","synthesis":"Compulsion and deterrence are distinct operations: one pushes a body toward an act, the other stops recurrence by displaying restraint and consequence. Together they define the non-voluntary alternatives against which chosen path-taking is read.","ayah_refs":["73:12","73:14","73:17","73:19"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Paths of Approach, Livelihood, and Combat","parent_semantic_invariant":"A way becomes meaningful through its destination and the kind of effort conducted along it.","parent_surprising_reach":"The same route system supports spiritual approach, commerce, scouting, return from travel, and military advance, with landmarks and lateral choices preventing “way” from remaining abstract.","key":"C","name":"Advancing along a battle route","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"A force scouts ahead, mobilizes, advances with courage, and engages an opposing side.","active_motifs":"reciprocal combat (`quranic:root_001200:B011/m01`); incitement toward battle (`quranic:root_000906:B017/m01`); advance scouts (`quranic:root_000138:B008/m01`); preparation for war (`quranic:root_000545:B004/m01`); courageous advance (`quranic:root_001207:B006/m01`); clamor of fighters and fire (`quranic:root_001434:B001/m01`); a traversable way (`quranic:root_000672:B001/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:14 `تَرْجُفُ`; 73:19-20 `سَبِيلًا/يَضْرِبُونَ/يَبْتَغُونَ/يُقَٰتِلُونَ/سَبِيلِ`; surface anchor unavailable for `quranic:root_001434:B001` because `م ع ع` is missing from root coverage","synthesis":"Scouting precedes mobilization, incitement initiates forward motion, courage sustains it, and reciprocal combat supplies the terminal encounter. The path is the organizing corridor for the whole sequence.","ayah_refs":["73:14","73:19"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Illness, Accommodation, and Restorative Care","parent_semantic_invariant":"When health or operative strength fails, care changes the task’s conditions and opens a path from constriction toward relief.","parent_surprising_reach":"Accommodation is materialized through nursing, relapse, infected wounds, reduced movement, breathing room, and a measure fitted to the patient.","key":"A","name":"Illness progressing under care","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"A body leaves equilibrium, movement weakens, a wound worsens or relapses, and an attendant works toward recovery.","active_motifs":"departure from bodily health (`quranic:root_001415:B001/m01`); tending a patient (`quranic:root_001415:B003/m01`); weakened action and curtailed movement (`quranic:root_001415:B004/m01`); dimness and loss of clarity (`quranic:root_001415:B005/m01`); illness taking the body (`quranic:root_000018:B007/m01`); relapse of illness or wound (`quranic:root_001096:B004/m01`); a suppurating sore (`quranic:root_000025:B011/m01`); a wound swelling toward corruption (`quranic:root_000138:B004/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:14,20 `ٱلْأَرْضُ/أَرْضِ`; 73:16,19 `أَخَذْنَٰهُ/ٱتَّخَذَ`; 73:20 `مَرْضَىٰ/يَبْتَغُونَ/غَفُورٌ`","synthesis":"The clinical scene distinguishes disease, impaired function, local infection, relapse, and caregiving. Care is an active counter-process, not merely sympathy for an already named patient.","ayah_refs":["73:14","73:16","73:20"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Provision Ecology","parent_semantic_invariant":"Provision moves through land, rain, crop, livestock, processing, and social distribution.","parent_surprising_reach":"The bundle assembles both a crop cycle and a dairy herd cycle beneath the surah’s contrast between luxury, punitive food, livelihood, and charitable giving.","key":"A","name":"Rain-fed crop and yield","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"Rain reaches fertile ground, seed is covered, shoots form ears, fruit ripens, and yield is gathered.","active_motifs":"fertile productive land (`quranic:root_000025:B002/m01`); covering seed with earth (`quranic:root_001307:B008/m01`); an ear of grain (`quranic:root_000672:B008/m01`); organic growth (`quranic:root_000637:B001/m01`); crop and fruit yield (`quranic:root_000009:B007/m01`); fruit reaching maturity (`quranic:root_000934:B005/m01`); rain exceeding ordinary need (`quranic:root_000138:B006/m01`); a river channel through land (`quranic:root_001559:B001/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:13 `طَعَامًا`; 73:14,20 `ٱلْأَرْضُ/أَرْضِ`; 73:17 `كَفَرْتُمْ`; 73:19-20 `سَبِيلًا/سَبِيلِ`; 73:20 `ءَاتُوا/زَّكَوٰةَ/يَبْتَغُونَ/ٱلنَّهَارَ`","synthesis":"Water, soil, seed cover, stalk, ripening, growth, and yield form a complete agricultural process. The same earth crossed by traders is also the productive substrate of the bounty they seek.","ayah_refs":["73:13","73:14","73:17","73:19","73:20"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Hearth and Meal Preparation","parent_semantic_invariant":"A meal is made by assembling a hearth, applying heat, cutting material, and controlling concentration.","parent_surprising_reach":"A pot, third hearthstone, lifting cloth, cut meat, fire, hastily baked dough, reduced drink, and measured sips form an ordinary kitchen whose operations are inverted by the punitive meal.","key":"A","name":"Building and working the hearth","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"A pot is set on three supports, heated, handled with a cloth, and supplied with cut food.","active_motifs":"cooking pot and its contents (`quranic:root_001205:B007/m01`); the third hearthstone completing a support (`quranic:root_000203:B007/m01`); cloth used to lower or lift a hot pot (`quranic:root_000248:B007/m01`); cutting meat (`quranic:root_001638:B002/m01`); exposure to cooking fire (`quranic:root_000879:B001/m01`); blazing heat (`quranic:root_000225:B001/m01`); dough baked before proper maturation (`quranic:root_001165:B006/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:11 `مَهِّلْهُمْ`; 73:12 `جَحِيمًا`; 73:14 `ٱلْجِبَالُ`; 73:17 `يَجْعَلُ`; 73:18 `مُنفَطِرٌ`; 73:20 `ثُلُثَهُ/يُقَدِّرُ/صَّلَوٰةَ`","synthesis":"Each motif occupies a kitchen role: supports stabilize the vessel, fire heats it, cloth protects the handler, cutting portions the ingredient, and premature baking names failed timing. The scene supplies the ordinary productive counterpart to infernal heat.","ayah_refs":["73:11","73:12","73:14","73:17","73:18","73:20"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Prestige, Luxury, and Its Reversal","parent_semantic_invariant":"Social distinction is constructed through comfort, rank, reputation, display, and claims of superiority, then exposed to reversal.","parent_surprising_reach":"Crown, leaders-as-mountains, fame, padded bodily display, dispersal, abasement, and scarcity make luxury a fragile social construction rather than a stable possession.","key":"B","name":"Display, fame, and arrogant enlargement","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"Status is amplified through bodily display, crown, public fame, elite leadership, and explicit superiority claims.","active_motifs":"arrogance and self-enlargement (`quranic:root_001029:B006/m01`); padding used to enlarge bodily appearance (`quranic:root_001029:B008/m01`); crown as a covering of kingship (`quranic:root_001307:B015/m01`); good fame spreading (`quranic:root_000745:B008/m01`); public reputation (`quranic:root_000516:B007/m01`); leaders likened to mountains (`quranic:root_000217:B012/m01`); claiming superiority over peers (`quranic:root_001163:B004/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:14 `ٱلْجِبَالُ`; 73:17 `كَفَرْتُمْ`; 73:18 `ٱلسَّمَآءُ`; 73:19 `تَذْكِرَةٌ`; 73:20 `فَضْلِ/أَعْظَمَ`","synthesis":"Status is literally made larger, covered by a crown, broadcast as fame, and stabilized by mountain-like leaders. The scene exposes prestige as crafted display and social repetition, not intrinsic magnitude.","ayah_refs":["73:14","73:17","73:18","73:19","73:20"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Prestige, Luxury, and Its Reversal","parent_semantic_invariant":"Social distinction is constructed through comfort, rank, reputation, display, and claims of superiority, then exposed to reversal.","parent_surprising_reach":"Crown, leaders-as-mountains, fame, padded bodily display, dispersal, abasement, and scarcity make luxury a fragile social construction rather than a stable possession.","key":"C","name":"Dispersal and abasement","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"A privileged collective loses cohesion, status, and secure abundance under a severe event.","active_motifs":"ostrich-like dispersal and loss of collective strength (`quranic:root_001525:B008/m01`); scarcity and abasement (`quranic:root_001251:B001/m01`); others replacing or following the first (`quranic:root_000019:B001/m01`); a calamitous event (`quranic:root_001700:B003/m01`); harmful consequence (`quranic:root_001619:B002/m01`); baseness and lowered rank (`quranic:root_000493:B003/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:11 `ٱلنَّعْمَةِ/قَلِيلًا`; 73:14,17 `يَوْمَ/يَوْمًا`; 73:16 `وَبِيلًا`; 73:20 `ءَاخَرُونَ/أَدْنَىٰ`","synthesis":"Reversal acts socially as well as materially: the group scatters, the first gives way to others, abundance contracts, and rank falls. This process answers the apparent permanence of luxury.","ayah_refs":["73:11","73:14","73:16","73:20"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Governance, Levy, and Deterrent Liability","parent_semantic_invariant":"Rule maintains a household or polity through care, contracts, assessed transfers, testimony, and sanctions that establish responsibility.","parent_surprising_reach":"The Pharaoh precedent activates not only kingship but a full administrative frame of household allegiance, tribute, tax, covenant, wage, surety, and exemplary punishment.","key":"A","name":"Household rule and sustaining authority","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"A ruler or household head gathers dependents, governs their affairs, and assumes responsibility for their maintenance.","active_motifs":"household and followers returning to a head (`quranic:root_000067:B003/m01`); governance and administrative repair (`quranic:root_000067:B004/m01`); ownership and sovereignty (`quranic:root_000532:B001/m01`); nurture and completion (`quranic:root_000532:B002/m01`); care and rule (`quranic:root_001273:B004/m01`); surety for another (`quranic:root_001332:B003/m01`); possessor or holder of a quality (`quranic:root_000527:B001/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:11 `أُو۟لِى`; 73:14,18,20 `كَانَتِ/كَانَ/يَكُونُ`; 73:19-20 `رَبِّهِ/رَبَّكَ`; surface anchor unavailable for `quranic:root_000527:B001` because `ذ و و` is missing from root coverage","synthesis":"Rule is rooted in a returning household relation, then specified as care, repair, maintenance, and surety. The possessor construction adds the status by which authority and privilege are socially attributed.","ayah_refs":["73:11","73:14","73:19"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Mobilization, Capture, and Mortal Outcome","parent_semantic_invariant":"Organized conflict moves from preparation and advance to close engagement, capture, subjugation, and death.","parent_surprising_reach":"The battle field includes rumor-driven mobilization, war clamor, throat holds, taming, clubs, exposure to killing, and the extinction of a group.","key":"A","name":"Mobilization and engagement","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"A force prepares, is incited, advances with courage, and meets an opposing force in reciprocal combat.","active_motifs":"preparation for war (`quranic:root_000545:B004/m01`); war at its fiercest (`quranic:root_000225:B002/m01`); incitement to fight (`quranic:root_000906:B017/m01`); reciprocal combat (`quranic:root_001200:B011/m01`); courageous forward movement (`quranic:root_001207:B006/m01`); clamor of warriors (`quranic:root_001434:B001/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:12 `جَحِيمًا`; 73:14 `تَرْجُفُ`; 73:20 `يَضْرِبُونَ/يُقَٰتِلُونَ/تُقَدِّمُوا`; surface anchor unavailable for `quranic:root_001434:B001` because `م ع ع` is missing from root coverage","synthesis":"Preparation and incitement precede advance; advance culminates in a two-sided encounter. Fire and clamor make mobilization audible and environmental, not only strategic.","ayah_refs":["73:12","73:14","73:20"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Animal Drive in Racing and Breeding","parent_semantic_invariant":"Animal force is expressed as directed forward drive, whether toward a finish, a mate, or productive offspring.","parent_surprising_reach":"Messenger-sending, precedence, ease, deterrence, and productive increase are all pressured by a detailed equine and herd mechanics.","key":"A","name":"Gait, racing order, and the sore hoof","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"A horse is urged into smooth movement, exposes its withers, advances in race order, and may shorten stride to protect a sore hoof.","active_motifs":"smooth easy gait (`quranic:root_000563:B003/m01`); successive detachments (`quranic:root_000563:B005/m01`); the horse’s muzzle and requested run (`quranic:root_000934:B009/m01`); elevated withers beneath the rider’s hand (`quranic:root_001285:B003/m01`); a prancing horse (`quranic:root_000138:B007/m01`); a camel advancing by its foreleg gait (`quranic:root_000009:B009/m01`); courageous advance (`quranic:root_001207:B006/m01`); light responsive movement (`quranic:root_001694:B005/m01`); protecting a painful hoof (`quranic:root_001677:B003/m01`); the second horse following the winner (`quranic:root_000879:B006/m01`); an animal running and then stopping (`quranic:root_001290:B007/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:11 `ٱلْمُكَذِّبِينَ`; 73:13 `طَعَامًا`; 73:14 `كَثِيبًا`; 73:15-16 `أَرْسَلْنَا/رَسُولًا/وَبِيلًا`; 73:17 `تَتَّقُونَ`; 73:20 `يَسَّرَ/تُقَدِّمُوا/صَّلَوٰةَ`","synthesis":"The scene specifies body part, handling point, gait, order, motivation, and injury response. Ease becomes a quality of movement, while stopping can signal either failure or protective adaptation.","ayah_refs":["73:11","73:13","73:14","73:15","73:17","73:20"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Cutting, Shaping, and Material Finish","parent_semantic_invariant":"Raw material becomes a finished object through cutting, striking, grinding, weaving, covering, and surface treatment.","parent_surprising_reach":"The language of action and fulfilled promise activates an artisan workshop containing shears, adze, mallet, grinding stone, textile, pile, coating, and tanning dose.","key":"A","name":"The shaping workshop","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"Workers cut and strike material, hew wood, grind solids, and weave a stable form.","active_motifs":"cutting with shears or teeth (`quranic:root_001217:B001/m01`); adze for shaping wood (`quranic:root_001207:B008/m01`); grinding stone (`quranic:root_000879:B008/m01`); workers including carpenter and digger (`quranic:root_001167:B003/m01`); heavy mallet or beating beam (`quranic:root_001619:B004/m01`); well-made weaving (`quranic:root_000217:B007/m01`); tools, striking place, and tent-making (`quranic:root_000906:B014/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:14 `ٱلْجِبَالُ`; 73:16 `وَبِيلًا`; 73:18 `مَفْعُولًا`; 73:20 `يَضْرِبُونَ/صَّلَوٰةَ/قَرْضًا/تُقَدِّمُوا`","synthesis":"Cutting establishes edges, adze and mallet shape mass, grinding reduces particles, and weaving assembles strands. Worker and tool roles make this a single production floor rather than a tool list.","ayah_refs":["73:14","73:16","73:18","73:20"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Cutting, Shaping, and Material Finish","parent_semantic_invariant":"Raw material becomes a finished object through cutting, striking, grinding, weaving, covering, and surface treatment.","parent_surprising_reach":"The language of action and fulfilled promise activates an artisan workshop containing shears, adze, mallet, grinding stone, textile, pile, coating, and tanning dose.","key":"B","name":"Textile covering and surface treatment","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"A woven surface receives nap, coating, dye, drape, or tanning treatment that changes appearance and durability.","active_motifs":"basic covering (`quranic:root_001307:B001/m01`); raised nap or hair on cloth (`quranic:root_001096:B003/m01`); draped garment (`quranic:root_001163:B005/m01`); a small tanning dose (`quranic:root_001533:B007/m01`); dyed cloth whose appearance deceives (`quranic:root_001290:B009/m01`); well-made weaving (`quranic:root_000217:B007/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:11 `ٱلْمُكَذِّبِينَ`; 73:14 `ٱلْجِبَالُ`; 73:17 `كَفَرْتُمْ`; 73:20 `فَضْلِ/أَنفُسِكُم/غَفُورٌ`","synthesis":"Weaving creates the substrate, nap and tanning alter its physical surface, dye changes its visual claim, and draping puts the finish into use. The deceptive cloth provides a concrete analogy for covering that misrepresents what lies beneath.","ayah_refs":["73:11","73:14","73:17","73:20"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Collective Assembly, Partition, and Dispersal","parent_semantic_invariant":"A collective can gather as a whole, divide into functional portions, move in successive groups, or lose cohesion.","parent_surprising_reach":"Crowd, herd, party, gathered whole, successive detachment, leftward branch, and ostrich-like scattering provide a precise social morphology.","key":"A","name":"Gathering into a whole","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"Individuals gather into a crowd or party whose members are treated as one operational whole.","active_motifs":"a party or group of people (`quranic:root_000957:B004/m01`); gathering parts into one (`quranic:root_001210:B001/m01`); a multitude likened to a mountain (`quranic:root_000217:B002/m01`); large gathered groups (`quranic:root_000532:B004/m01`); a people or social body (`quranic:root_001273:B001/m01`); the whole crowd without exception (`quranic:root_001096:B008/m01`); a place filled tightly by people (`quranic:root_001091:B002/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:11 `ٱلْمُكَذِّبِينَ`; 73:13 `غُصَّةٍ`; 73:14 `ٱلْجِبَالُ`; 73:19-20 `رَبِّهِ/رَبَّكَ/طَآئِفَةٌ/تَقُومُ/غَفُورٌ/قُرْءَانِ`","synthesis":"Gathering scales from party to people to mountain-like multitude, while crowding names the spatial consequence of density. “The whole crowd” closes the scene by removing residual outsiders.","ayah_refs":["73:11","73:13","73:14","73:19"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Making, Existing, and Transforming","parent_semantic_invariant":"A thing moves from possibility through creation and occurrence into a changed, identifiable state.","parent_surprising_reach":"Creation, existence, fabrication, and transformation are kept distinct, allowing the fulfilled promise and altered children or mountains to be read as different ontological operations.","key":"A","name":"Creating and bringing into existence","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"An agent originates a thing, which then occurs and stands as an existing object that can be known or found.","active_motifs":"making or creating (`quranic:root_000248:B001/m01`); originating and beginning creation (`quranic:root_001165:B002/m01`); occurrence in time (`quranic:root_001332:B001/m01`); location and standing in existence (`quranic:root_001332:B002/m01`); existence after absence (`quranic:root_001626:B002/m01`); a knowable and reportable thing (`quranic:root_000831:B001/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:14,18,20 `كَانَتِ/كَانَ/يَكُونُ`; 73:17 `يَجْعَلُ`; 73:18 `مُنفَطِرٌ`; 73:19 `شَآءَ`; 73:20 `تَجِدُوهُ`","synthesis":"Creation names agency, occurrence supplies time, place supplies position, and existence makes the result available to knowledge and report. The stages prevent “making” from becoming an undifferentiated synonym for being.","ayah_refs":["73:14","73:17","73:18","73:19","73:20"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Relinquishment and Transferred Handling","parent_semantic_invariant":"Releasing an affair changes who holds, answers for, restrains, or submits it.","parent_surprising_reach":"Leaving is not a single empty gesture: it can transfer accountability to a sovereign guarantor, recoil in weakness, impose restraint, disavow an attachment, or end in submission.","key":"A","name":"Leaving an affair to accountable custody","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"One actor releases an affair while another assumes ownership, accountability, and surety for its handling.","active_motifs":"leaving a thing (`quranic:root_001638:B003/m01`); holding a person accountable for fault (`quranic:root_000018:B002/m01`); ownership and sovereignty (`quranic:root_000532:B001/m01`); surety and undertaking another’s care (`quranic:root_001332:B003/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:11 `وَذَرْنِى`; 73:16,19 `أَخَذْنَٰهُ/ٱتَّخَذَ`; 73:19-20 `رَبِّهِ/رَبَّكَ`; 73:14,18,20 `كَانَتِ/كَانَ/يَكُونُ`","synthesis":"Relinquishment does not erase the affair; it moves it under a different holder. Accountability specifies judgment, sovereignty specifies authority, and surety specifies continued care, turning “leave me” into a transfer of operative control.","ayah_refs":["73:11","73:16","73:19","73:14"]},{"parent_channel":"P2 - Bodily Signs of Crisis","parent_semantic_invariant":"Acute crisis becomes legible through involuntary motion and visible changes in face, eye, lip, and hair.","parent_surprising_reach":"Cosmic shaking and infernal heat are reframed as somatic diagnostics: convulsion, pulse, fear-tremor, anger-flush, jutting eye, eye-film, cleft lip, facial distortion, and greying.","key":"A","name":"Convulsion and heated anger","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"Fear or anger produces tremor, involuntary motion, pulse, and heat visible at the face.","active_motifs":"a bodily tremor or shaking (`quranic:root_000025:B008/m01`); possessed involuntary head and body movement (`quranic:root_000025:B012/m01`); disturbed pulsing or agitation (`quranic:root_000906:B012/m01`); trembling from fear or anger (`quranic:root_001251:B005/m01`); felt anger (`quranic:root_001626:B005/m01`); a face blazing with anger (`quranic:root_000225:B004/m01`)","ayah_anchors":"73:11 `قَلِيلًا`; 73:12 `جَحِيمًا`; 73:14,20 `ٱلْأَرْضُ/أَرْضِ`; 73:20 `يَضْرِبُونَ/تَجِدُوهُ`","synthesis":"Tremor supplies the gross motion, pulse the internal rhythm, and anger the affective heat that reaches the face. The earth’s convulsion and the fire’s blaze are thereby materialized as a body losing voluntary stillness.","ayah_refs":["73:11","73:12","73:14","73:20"]}],"channel_generated_outputs":{"source_dir":"quran-data/data/analysis/channels/network-v3/s073","files":[{"path":"quran-data/data/analysis/channels/network-v3/s073/channel_candidates.jsonl","role":"channel_candidates.jsonl","bytes":8914018,"compressed":false},{"path":"quran-data/data/analysis/channels/network-v3/s073/channel_candidates.tsv","role":"channel_candidates.tsv","bytes":257927,"compressed":false},{"path":"quran-data/data/analysis/channels/network-v3/s073/summary.json","role":"summary.json","bytes":8709,"compressed":false},{"path":"quran-data/data/analysis/channels/network-v3/s073/families/candidate_graphs.jsonl","role":"families/candidate_graphs.jsonl","bytes":4915982,"compressed":false},{"path":"quran-data/data/analysis/channels/network-v3/s073/families/candidate_similarity_edges.tsv","role":"families/candidate_similarity_edges.tsv","bytes":122140,"compressed":false},{"path":"quran-data/data/analysis/channels/network-v3/s073/families/channel_families.jsonl","role":"families/channel_families.jsonl","bytes":862645,"compressed":false},{"path":"quran-data/data/analysis/channels/network-v3/s073/families/candidate_family_membership.tsv","role":"families/candidate_family_membership.tsv","bytes":11610,"compressed":false},{"path":"quran-data/data/analysis/channels/network-v3/s073/families/family_branch_inventory.tsv","role":"families/family_branch_inventory.tsv","bytes":306169,"compressed":false},{"path":"quran-data/data/analysis/channels/network-v3/s073/families/consolidation_summary.json","role":"families/consolidation_summary.json","bytes":9227,"compressed":false},{"path":"quran-data/data/analysis/channels/network-v3/s073/paths/path_summary.json","role":"paths/path_summary.json","bytes":16399,"compressed":false},{"path":"quran-data/data/analysis/channels/network-v3/s073/paths/path_families/semantic_path_families.jsonl","role":"paths/path_families/semantic_path_families.jsonl","bytes":30036552,"compressed ## Your response

Write in tr. Produce your response as three parts, in this order. If you return one combined response, label the parts clearly. If an orchestrator asks you to write the parts into separate files, omit wrapper labels from the prose and evidence files; the file path already supplies the label.

1. **The prose** — per the output contract in `COMMENTARY_SPEC.md` §5 and the task document above: continuous, single voice, no provenance markers, no headers named after evidence layers.
2. **The evidence surface** — separate from the prose, addressable per phrase, per `PRINCIPLES.md` §12.
3. A section headed exactly `=== PROMPT FRICTION ===` reporting honestly where the specification above was unclear, self-contradictory, underspecified, or impossible to follow, and any point where you had to invent a rule to proceed. Report this section every time, even if the run went cleanly — say so explicitly if you found nothing. This is how the specification gets improved.

## V2 Rendering Profile — 5.6 Sol High

This run tests whether `5.6-sol-high` can increase lexical depth while preserving
reader-facing clarity.

Increase depth through semantic precision, not additional bulk.

Before drafting, silently build a coverage ledger:

- include every `must_integrate` topic;
- include every `candidate` with a reader payoff not already expressed;
- if `v12_reader_responses` is absent because the default workflow retired
  per-ayah focus runs, do not infer a `stage_00` isolated response or
  confidence movement;
- if `v12_focus_trace_hermetic` is present, use its `baseline_models`,
  `context_deltas`, and `surprising_valid_outliers` as reconstructed
  before/after evidence. Do not call it a staged reveal transcript. Preserve
  surprising outliers when they remain anchored in this ayah, especially
  secondary split-root activations;
- include a regular reader-walk, plus/minus-5 reader-walk, whole-surah reading,
  or cross-run-publication item only when it adds a distinct retrospective
  insight or coverage check;
- use branch inventories to support explanations; they create no standalone
  prose obligation. When fallback inventories are present, treat them as
  restricted to this ayah's roots and anchored citations, not as a whole-surah
  obligation.
- treat `root_lexicon` as full dictionary/gloss support, not branch-filtered.
  It uses QAC-to-Furuq root mapping; when a QAC root is split, non-dominant
  Furuq targets are included as additional root entries and recorded in
  coverage. If multiple QAC roots map to the same Furuq root, the shared entry
  records all source roots in `qac_roots_ar` / `qac_root_mappings`; use those
  fields for evidence attribution when present, not the legacy single `root_ar`
  alone. Dictionary/gloss branches are evidence support, not independent
  obligations, unless tied to this ayah's word, topic, reader payoff, or cited
  relation. Full field means all distinct reader payoffs from activated
  material, not every dictionary branch for the root.
- if `channel_generated_outputs` lists quran-data files and this run gives you
  file access, read only those listed files when channel-family/path detail is
  necessary. Treat them as candidate/family/path evidence, not as an adjudicated
  channel ledger. State B channel restrictions still apply.

For each critical word, preserve these distinct layers when available:

1. local grammatical work;
2. locally selected sense;
3. coherent pressure supplied by activated or cited root branches;
4. one form, sound, rarity, or variant observation with a unique payoff;
5. later contextual change, compressed to its final reader-visible result.

Do not flatten these layers into a general metaphor. Deepen generic summaries by
naming the exact lexical mechanism. Ordering grammar and local meaning first is
grounding, not truth-ranking.

At first mention of an ayah word, use a structured Arabic surface span:
`{ar:surface_form, tr:Turkish-readable transliteration, gloss:target-language meaning}`.
Use the same full span again when the prose returns to that word after moving to
another word or another paragraph. Inside one short local sequence, after a full
span has just been given, a Turkish label or transliteration is enough. Explain
every technical term in the same sentence. Keep raw roots, root skeletons,
letter-by-letter root transliterations, and branch IDs in the evidence surface.
In prose, attach root discussion to the surface word:
`{ar:ٱلْعَادِيَاتِ, tr:el-âdiyât, gloss:koşup atılanlar} kelimesinin bağlı
olduğu kök alanı...`, not `ʿ-d-v kökü...`.

A paragraph may begin with the ayah's surface, a concrete image, or a
reader-facing claim. It may not begin with a bare grammar label or stacked
abstractions. Prefer "Âyet önce hamdi Allah'a verir; sabit ad cümlesi bu hamdi
yerleşik bir hüküm olarak taşır" over "Bu âyet, tek bir isim cümlesiyle yerleşik
bir hüküm kurar."

For this short ayah, keep at least two-thirds of the prose on its own wording.
Compress all later developments into at most three paragraphs and end with one
plain synthesis paragraph.

Use `v12_cross_run_publication`, if present, only as a compact coverage/priority
check derived from regular and plus/minus-5 reader runs. Do not copy it as prose,
and do not let it override local bundle evidence.

Use an explicit negative predicate only to correct a likely misconception,
protect the primary sense from replacement, or preserve live counter-evidence.
Default ceiling for this ayah: three. Never stack two negatives in one sentence.
During final revision, rewrite all other negatives as positive predication.
Do not drive negation to zero by default: if no explicit negative is needed,
include a profile-specific style audit in friction confirming that there was no
live misconception, replacement risk, or counter-evidence requiring one.

Pass only if every paragraph has one distinct reader payoff and the prose remains
clear with the evidence surface closed.
